## Prompt 1 — 2026-11-06 06:10:00 UTC

### User:

Please expand and organize these design notes into a technical plan to implement using Opus agents. Changes should not overlap between batches, to minimize conflicts when I review and merge. Splitting tasks is also an option. Challenge raise potential problems and get elaboration, to deliver me a brief summary of tasks that these subagents will do. They can go once I approve if everything looks right. This will be update 0.5.0.

Voronoi-based sky islands:
> Voronoi decisions in `CLAUDE.md` apply.
> Prior art, implementation for Aerolite must not copy or port:
	- 2022 Aether Prototype (I wrote this prototype implementation) `../The-Aether/src/main/java/com/gildedgames/aether/common/world/gen/chunk/CelledSpaceGenerator.java` (transcript citation: `https://github.com/Drullkus/The-Aether-Experimental/blob/feat/drullkus/chunkgenexperimental/src/main/java/com/gildedgames/aether/common/world/gen/chunk/CelledSpaceGenerator.java`)
	- Relict (Older agents wrote this basing off of the 2022 Aether prototype chunk generator above) `../IdeaProjects/Relict/src/main/java/us/drullk/relict/worldgen/VoronoiSource.java` (transcript citation: `https://github.com/Drullkus/Relict/blob/main/src/main/java/us/drullk/relict/worldgen/VoronoiSource.java`)
	- Inigo Quilez's smooth voronoi (Awesome guy) `https://iquilezles.org/articles/smoothvoronoi/`
- World Type in world creation screen, with its own settings accessible via settings button.
	- Preview editor, separate from the Amazing Pillars. The two should co-exist in gameplay, resulting in land chunks suspended by infinite pillars. Analogous to decimated reinforced concrete.
- Few cells don't get islands.
- Information needed per voronoi cell as density functions to shape the island:
	- Cell Centroid (the Delauney coordinate)
	- Cell's center-of-mass (or just center)
	- Distance from center
	- Distance from Y-axis of center
	- Distance from XZ-plane of center
	> SVG prototype using image-based elaboration that points out the zero-space for these.
	> Distance functions probably need some kind of combinator, choosable in the data structure.
	> Option to smooth distances so that they're not hard polygon edges but instead rounded off. Slider goes between hard corners verses complete continuity.
	> Islands have their inner voronoi space, such as for choosing biomes, density functions, or structure placement. The 2022 Aether prototype was working to this end.
	> Example function for island mountain can be `0 < (1-height)^2 - width` for its top. Bottom takes same shape, upside down.
- Avoid loading too much functionality into a custom chunk generator. In fact, avoid it outright. Relict has it just for cache savings. Prefer density functions.
- Abstract the voronoi source into its own registry and then making density functions and biome source reference that registered source. Use a test to verify that the source is singular between the density functions and biome source, so that the cache better de-duplicates.
- Possible island types:
	- Asteroid.
	- Lake bowl: Entire voronoi cell or the voronoi cell has an inner 3D voronoi space that creates a blobby shape. Not too dissimiar to a chopped coconut.
- Relocate structures to centers of islands: Villages, Strongholds, Trial Chambers, Trail Ruins, etc.
	- Limit one surface and one underground structure per island (or island sub-cell)
	- Will NeoForge's structure modifiers work for this?

### Claude:

**Findings before the questions**

- NeoForge structure modifiers cannot move a structure. `ModifiableStructureInfo` holds only the structure settings: biomes, spawn overrides, step and terrain adaptation. The placement is in the structure set, and NeoForge 21.1 has no modifier for structure sets.
- A density function gets no world seed. `RandomState` gives the seed only to vanilla noise and to the vanilla End island function. The Relict source reads the seed from the running server, so it cannot work in a preview.
- `/locate`, explorer maps and eyes of ender find only the vanilla random-spread and concentric-rings placements. A new placement type is invisible to them unless it extends the random-spread placement.
- Village pieces follow the heightmap. A piece over the void lands at the bottom of the world.
- The void Overworld has sea level -64. So a lake bowl gets no water from the noise step, and mineshafts move to the bottom of the world.
- The vanilla surface rules need the router field `initial_density_without_jaggedness`. With the current zero value, no island gets grass.
- `AeroliteWorldgenEntries` and `AeroliteLanguageProvider` are single files. Two batches that add entries or lang keys there overlap.

**Earth photographs** (I read the file names from the Wikimedia category pages. I did not view the images.)

- [Mount Roraima, Venezuela](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Roraima3_(79).JPG): tepui, a flat top with cliffs on all sides.
- [Mayon, Philippines](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Mayon_Volcano_and_the_rice_paddies.jpg): concave cone, the profile of `(1-height)^2 - width`.
- [Kerið, Iceland](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Kerid_crater,_Iceland_in_2015.JPG): crater lake in a small bowl.
- [Crater Lake, Oregon](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Crater_Lake_aerial.jpg): caldera lake with a full rim.
- [Glacial erratic, Marion](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Glacial_erratic_Marion.jpg): isolated boulder. An asteroid has no Earth landform, so this is the nearest one.

```
Decisions of 2026-11-05 for Aerolite 0.5.0 (owner answered eleven question rounds). The batch summary waits for the owner's approval; no agent ran yet when this was written.

Architecture:
- New preset `aerolite:islands`, "Aerolite Islands", next to `aerolite:void`. No custom chunk generator. Vanilla noise generators with Aerolite density functions.
- Island slider values live in a world data pack: the editor writes registry entries into the temp data pack folder of the create world screen, Done reloads the registries. One set of values per dimension.
- Seed: the owner chose `ServerLifecycleHooks.getCurrentServer().overworld().getSeed()`. The preview binds its own seed like the pillar preview (seed field, Done writes it back). My interpretation: the preview and JUnit pass the seed explicitly.
- Two Voronoi levels (island cells, inner 3D cells) plus a seeded neighbor merge. Merge needs a flag in both island types; types stay, shapes blend through the combinator.
- Distance functions: origin (site or center of mass), unit (normalized or blocks) and smoothing are fields of each density function. Combinators in data: hard union, smooth union, intersection, subtraction. Their purpose is to join several cells into one jagged landmass.
- Density functions decide which inner cells are solid (for example distance from the island center, or "rocky cubes").
- Island types are a datapack registry, datagenned, open to modpackers. A type holds: shape density function, named number parameters (editor sliders), inner cell switch and scale, merge flag, biome list in named climate groups, weighted structure set list, structure unit (island cell or inner cell).
- Shipped types: landmass Flat, Hills, Mountain, Mesa steps (profiles are density functions), Asteroid, Lake bowl, Donut, Biosphere.
- Biomes: inner cells pick biomes from the list of their island type; a low-frequency noise picks the climate group of a region; a simplex noise ripples the inner cell borders per QuartPos.
- Water, glass and bridges are painters of the Aerolite chunk status. Walker trees of worldgen are a feature, walks cut to the writable area. Tree chance per segment is a new field of the pillar variation with an editor slider.
- Bridge deck follows a Bresenham line between the two island edges.
- Structures: mixin on the structure state wraps placements (wrapper extends random spread so /locate works). Each unit walks the weighted structure list of its island type in a seeded weighted shuffle; first fit wins; fit = biome tag of the structure plus a size check. One surface and one underground structure per unit; two Aerolite tags name the class by structure identity. Unlisted sets: fallback island list of the dimension source, else vanilla placement. Underground depth: data overrides with a guarded height provider (vanilla height outside island dimensions); a second mixin moves mineshaft and stronghold pieces.
- Editor: vanilla screens plus ImGui panel; value groups: source values, island placement, island type list, cloud height. HTML mock first.
- Preview: separate from the pillar preview; shared core split first; shows surface rule blocks, pillars overlay, water and glass, and a Voronoi wireframe (white cell edges, translucent face grids with thinner lines).
- Process: prototypes, owner review, then Java batches defined in detail. At most 6 agents at once. CLAUDE.md changes in the last Java batch. Numeric defaults (empty cell chance, merge chance, inner scale, ripple) are picked at the prototype review.

**Why:** The owner picked these in chat; the repo holds no record until the branches merge and CLAUDE.md is updated.
```

### Claude:

# Aerolite 0.5.0 plan

Nothing in the repository changed, and no agent ran. You chose prototypes, then review, then Java, so approval starts prototype wave 1 only.

## Decisions that shape the batches

- **Preset:** `aerolite:islands`, "Aerolite Islands", next to Aerolite Void. Vanilla noise generators only.
- **Voronoi:** two levels (island cells and inner 3D cells) plus a seeded neighbor merge. Two cells merge only when both island types allow it. Each cell keeps its type, and the combinator blends the shapes.
- **Density functions:** origin, unit and smoothing are fields of each function. The combinators are hard union, smooth union, intersection and subtraction.
- **Island type:** a datapack registry entry that holds:
  - the shape function and the named parameters, which are the editor sliders;
  - the inner cell switch and scale, and the merge flag;
  - the biome list in named climate groups;
  - the weighted structure list and the structure unit.
- **Shipped types:** Flat, Hills, Mountain, Mesa steps, Asteroid, Lake bowl, Donut, Biosphere.
- **Storage:** the editor writes a world data pack, with one set of values per dimension. Done reloads the registries.
- **Structures:**
  - A mixin on the structure state wraps each placement.
  - Each unit walks the structure list of its island type in a seeded weighted shuffle. The first structure that fits wins.
  - A structure fits when its biome tag accepts the unit biome and the island passes the size check.
  - A guarded height provider sets the depth of jigsaw structures. A second mixin moves mineshaft and stronghold pieces.
- **Painters:** water, glass and bridges are painters of the Aerolite chunk status. Trees are a feature. A bridge deck follows a Bresenham line.

## Prototype wave 1 (starts on approval, 4 Opus agents)

| Batch | Branch | Folder | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | `claude/voronoi-fields` | `prototypes/voronoi/`, `prototypes/tools/`, `prototypes/shadertoy/` | SVG sheets of one slice: site, center of mass and the zero space of each distance. Further sheets show both units, the smoothing steps, the four combinators, the merge and the biome ripple. One GLSL file with its Shadertoy port, and the GLSL validator. |
| P2 | `claude/islands-ui-mock` | `prototypes/ui/islands/` | HTML mock of the island editor: vanilla screens and the ImGui panel. |
| P3 | `claude/walker-prototype` | `prototypes/walker/` | Standalone render of the walk, the branches, the maturity rule and the hue cycle on a pillar. |
| P4 | `claude/bridge-prototype` | `prototypes/bridges/` | Standalone render of the Bresenham deck, the two widths, the rails and the material tables. |

## Prototype wave 2 (after you merge P1, 3 Opus agents)

| Batch | Folder | Task |
|---|---|---|
| P5 | `prototypes/landmass/` | Flat, Hills, Mountain and Mesa steps as signed distance functions, with inner cells on and off. |
| P6 | `prototypes/asteroid/`, `prototypes/lake-bowl/` | The asteroid and the lake bowl with its water level. |
| P7 | `prototypes/donut/`, `prototypes/biosphere/` | The donut and the biosphere with its dome shapes. |

Each shape file gets a Shadertoy port with its own file name. You pick the number defaults at the review: the empty cell chance, the merge chance, the inner cell scale and the ripple.

## Java outline (I define the batches in detail after your review)

| Wave | Batches, each with its own packages |
|---|---|
| A | Voronoi source and island type data model with registries and tests. Split of the preview core. Painter list for the chunk status, plus contributor lists for lang and worldgen entries. Walker tree block, seed, game rule and Veil render. |
| B | Density functions and combinators. Voronoi biome source with the single-source test. Structure placement with its two mixins. Editor settings model and the world data pack writer. |
| C | Shipped island types and the preset. Water and glass painter. Bridge painter. Tree feature and the tree chance field of the variation. Vanilla editor screens. Island preview with the panel and the wireframe. |
| D | Integration with GameTests. CLAUDE.md rewrite. |

Wave A makes the shared files open to additions first. After it, each batch adds its own files only.

## Problems that stay open

- **Seed:** `getCurrentServer().overworld()` gives null until the levels exist. The preview and the JUnit tests have no server, so they pass their seed to the source directly.
- **Fit test:** the placement predicts the fit from the biome and the size. A structure that fails for another reason leaves its unit empty.
- **Height overrides:** the guarded height provider replaces vanilla structure files in the mod data. Another mod that replaces the same file wins or loses by pack sequence.
- **Dedicated server:** it has no editor. It uses the shipped entries or a data pack that the admin adds.
- **Trees:** a feature cuts each walk at the 3x3 chunk area. The tree draw must not use a pillar random, or `ShaftLayoutTest` fails.
- **Sea level:** it stays at -64. A higher value fills the void below Y -54 with lava.
- **Two cautions in CLAUDE.md:** it names `prototypes/tools/` and `prototypes/shadertoy/`, and the repository has neither. P1 creates both.

### Claude:

No agent started. Wave 1 holds for your explicit approval.

Your five clarifications change four Java batches. They change no prototype batch.

## Changes by clarification

| Clarification | Batch | Change |
|---|---|---|
| Server seed only for real generation | Voronoi source (wave A) | The source reads `getCurrentServer().overworld()` only on the world generation path. The source also takes a seed as a direct input. |
| Preview seed like the pillar preview | Island preview (wave C) | The preview rolls a seed on entry when the seed field is empty. It gives that seed to the source directly. Done writes the seed text to the create world screen. |
| Fit test accepted, structures can extend past the island | Structure placement (wave B) | The size check compares a needed radius from the data with the solid radius of the unit. It never cuts a piece. A structure can have a needed radius smaller than its real size, so an entrance or an airship dock can pass the rim. |
| Dedicated server uses shipped defaults | Editor settings model (wave B) | No server-side writer of the world data pack exists. A dedicated server without a data pack and without a save generates from the shipped entries. |
| Tree feature uses the feature random | Tree feature (wave C) | The feature draws every tree value from its feature random. It reads no pillar random, so the fingerprint of `ShaftLayoutTest` stays. The tree chance field of the variation is data only. |
| No lava in the void | Shipped island types and preset (wave C), integration (wave D) | The preset batch keeps each sea level at a value that gives no lava. Integration adds a GameTest that finds no lava block in a void chunk and none below an island. |

## Lava facts from the vanilla source

- `NoiseBasedChunkGenerator.createFluidPicker` gives lava below `min(-54, seaLevel)`.
- The Overworld starts at Y -64. So its sea level stays at -64, and no block lies below the lava level.
- The Nether and the End start at Y 0. So they get no lava from the fluid picker at any sea level.
- The vanilla cave carvers write lava in carved space near the bottom of the world. An island that reaches that depth can get carver lava. The GameTest covers this case.

## One point for the prototypes

- `glslangValidator` is not on this machine. The Relict validator runs in Docker. P1 checks for Docker and reports the result.

The wave tables of the earlier summary stay as they are.

### Claude:

The walker tree prototype is complete and checked. The other three wave 1 agents still run.

## Walker tree: `claude/walker-prototype`

- I ran `python3 walker.py --check`. It reports 0 errors over 6 variants of 2000 trees each.
- I looked at three of the nine images: the direction variants, the tree chance sheet and the chunk edge sheet. Each one shows what the README says.
- The diff holds only `prototypes/walker/`, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree. The branch stays.
- I did not open the orbit viewer `index.html`. The agent loaded its first scene only and did not test the controls.

```bash
git cherry-pick -n main..claude/walker-prototype
```

After the cherry-pick, the images are in `prototypes/walker/images/`, and the README lists the commands.

## Rule problems that the prototype found

- **The 32-step limit never acts.** The longest walk in 60000 trees has 23 steps. The mean walk has 8 steps.
- **The hue circle is rarely full.** About 6% of the trees pass through all 16 hues. Most trees show red to green only.
- **The cut is rare.** The root chunk is the center of the 3x3 area, so the cut needs a reach of 16 blocks. It ends 0.21% of the trees.
- **The trees depend on the feature sequence.** Neighbor features write into one shared area. A reversed sequence changes 1 to 5 of 200 scenes. So a tree is not a pure function of the seed.

## Your choices from the images

The README section "Choices for the owner" lists 12 points. These five have side-by-side images:

| Point | Variants | Image |
|---|---|---|
| Direction | The walk keeps its direction, or it picks a new one at each step | `point_direction.png` |
| Collision | Any air block, or the chorus rule | `point_collision.png` |
| First step | Along the face normal, or any free direction | `point_first.png` |
| Cut | The walk stops with a seed, or it turns back inside | `point_cut.png` |
| Branch length | A branch inherits the length of its parent, or it starts at zero | `point_branch.png` |

The other seven points have no image:

- the step count of the first block, 0 or 1;
- the maturity chance, length / 32 or its square;
- roots on the bottom face;
- roots on any outer block or on maze wall blocks only;
- the tree chance slider, proposed as 0% to 100% in steps of 5% with a default of 25%;
- one tree per segment or more;
- the feature sequence problem: accept it, or make each tree ignore the blocks of the other trees.

### Claude:

The bridge prototype is complete and checked. The Voronoi fields agent and the island editor mock agent still run.

## Bridge: `claude/bridge-prototype`

- I ran `python3 prototypes/bridges/bridges.py --check`. It prints `PASS` over 672 bridges.
- The measured material shares match the tables of CLAUDE.md within 0.5 percentage points.
- I looked at three of the 14 images: `vertical.png`, `meet.png` and `width_railed.png`. Each one shows what the README says.
- The diff holds only `prototypes/bridges/`, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree. The branch stays.

```bash
git cherry-pick -n main..claude/bridge-prototype
```

## Rule problems that the prototype found

- **The step onto the island is too high.** With the deck that stops at the island, 21% of the bridges end in a step above the limit, up to 1.5 blocks. With the notch, every bridge ends in a wall of up to 4.5 blocks. Both variants need a rule for that last step.
- **A railed deck at 45 degrees can be closed.** In the `column` variant, the walkway cells touch only at corners, so the walker model cannot cross. The `corner` and `distance` variants stay open.
- **A 3-wide deck at an angle has a stepped edge.** A player can step off at a diagonal notch.
- **A bridge between merged cells is pointless.** The `keep` variant gives a 2-step deck or no deck.
- **The rim end points cost more.** The search scans every column of both islands, and each chunk needs the result. The Java code needs a cache per island pair.

## Your choices from the images

| Point | Variants | Image |
|---|---|---|
| Vertical resolution | Whole blocks with lower slabs, or half blocks with lower and upper slabs | `vertical.png`, `heights.png` |
| End points | The center line, or the nearest rim pair | `endpoints.png` |
| Width rasterization | `column`, `corner` or `distance` | `width_narrow.png`, `width_railed.png` |
| Deck meets island | Stop at the island, or cut a notch | `meet.png` |
| Merged cells | No bridge, or a bridge | `merged.png` |
| Limits | Proposed: 2 to 64 steps, slope up to 1.0 for whole blocks and 0.5 for half blocks | `limits.png` |

Three points have no image:

- the full block under an End stone rail, end stone or end stone bricks;
- the full block under a purpur rail, which the prototype sets to the purpur block;
- a rail cell whose deck position is island solid, which the prototype leaves out.

## Limits of the check

- The chunk check reuses one end point plan per bridge. So it does not prove that the end point search is pure per chunk.
- The walker is a grid model. It does not use Minecraft collision.
- The islands are stand-ins. The real island shapes can change the entry steps.

## One observation in the main checkout

- `prototypes/walker/` held untracked files for a moment during my check. Now it holds only the empty folder `images/`, and `git status` is clean. I did not write there and I did not delete it.

### Claude:

The island editor mock is complete and checked. Only the Voronoi fields agent still runs.

## Island editor mock: `claude/islands-ui-mock`

- I served the branch from a temporary local server and opened both pages. The console shows no error on either page.
- `panel.html` shows the three layouts side by side at 360 units.
- `screens.html` shows the Customize screen. The arrow of the Overworld row opens the dimension screen with its three tabs.
- The diff holds only `prototypes/ui/islands/`, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree, stopped the server and deleted the temporary launch file. The branch stays.
- The agent drove the sliders, the push rules, the add rows and the Remove buttons by script. I did not repeat that test.
- No one tested the pages from `file://` in a real browser. The Preview hand-over between the two pages and the Paste buttons are also untested there.

```bash
git cherry-pick -n main..claude/islands-ui-mock
```

After the cherry-pick, open `prototypes/ui/islands/panel.html` and `prototypes/ui/islands/screens.html`.

## What the mock shows

- **Panel:** the Preview section, then one header per dimension with the groups Voronoi Source, Island Placement and Island Types, then a dimension add row.
- **Layout A:** one box per island type, like the pillar variations.
- **Layout B:** one tab per island type. The tab bar scrolls.
- **Layout C:** one line per type with its weight, and one editor box for the selected line.
- **Screens:** the Customize screen with a pillar-style add row, one row per dimension, the cloud slider and the footer Done, Preview, Cancel. The dimension screen has one tab per value group.
- **Game view:** a 2D top view stand-in, not the 3D preview.

## Your choices

The README lists 18 points. These points change the Java work most:

| Point | Variants |
|---|---|
| Island type list layout | A, B or C |
| Dimension rows | A pillar-style add row with prefab lists and remove buttons, or a fixed row per dimension |
| Prefab registry | A new registry `aerolite:island_list`, or no prefabs |
| Type entries | One entry per type id, or repeated types |
| Named values of a parameter | A slider over the names, a combo, or no named values |
| Cloud slider in the panel | The Preview section, the Overworld header, or not in the panel |
| Inner cell scale | A text field or a slider |
| Copy and Paste per type entry | Keep or drop |

The agent added the add row, the prefab lists and the per-type Copy and Paste by its own decision. You did not ask for them.

The other points are labels, ranges, steps and defaults. All numbers are proposals, and the example types and parameters are invented.

## Items that clip at 240 units

- The seed field with a 19-digit seed.
- Each dimension header with its Remove button.
- "All dimensions configured" in the dimension combo.
- Parameter sliders with long named values, for example "Dome Shape: Dodecahedron".

Nothing clips at 360 or 600 units.

### Claude:

Prototype wave 1 is complete. The Voronoi fields branch is checked, and it was the last of the four.

## Voronoi fields: `claude/voronoi-fields`

- I ran `python3 prototypes/voronoi/fields.py --check`. It prints `ALL CHECKS PASS`.
- I ran the GLSL validator in Docker. The `.frag` file and the Shadertoy port pass, and the port matches the marked lines of the `.frag` file.
- I looked at three of the eight SVG sheets (2a, 5 and 6) and three of the ten shader renders. Each one shows what the README says.
- The commit holds only its own paths, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree. The branch stays.
- No one ran the port on shadertoy.com. Only the validator and a software renderer ran it.

```bash
git cherry-pick -n main..claude/voronoi-fields
```

After the cherry-pick, the sheets and the renders are in `prototypes/voronoi/images/`.

## Defects in the candidate math

The agent found ten defects. Each one has a fix on the sheets and in the README. These five change the design:

| Defect | Fix on the sheets |
|---|---|
| A smooth max is above 1 on the cell wall, so a smoothed island shrinks. | Divide by the least value of the smooth field on the wall. |
| `(1-height)^2` rises again above height 1. This puts a mirrored solid above and below the cell. | Use `max(0, 1-height)^2`. |
| The merge candidate leaves a gap in 120 of 217 face pairs at fill 0.6. It leaks out of both cells in 4 pairs. | A fill threshold that rises to 1 toward the shared face, with a neck exponent `q`. |
| A smooth union alone closes the gap only at a large width. | The same threshold rule. |
| The biome ripple folds when its amplitude is large against its scale. | Keep the amplitude below the scale. Sheet 7 shows the fold. |

## Limits of the shader

- Its hash differs from the Python hash, so its layout differs from the sheets.
- It has no wall normalization. At full smoothing, its islands are small round blobs.
- `render-merge.png` shows thin white lines along the merged seams. I do not know if they are march errors or real gaps.
- No render uses the center of mass origin, the intersection or the subtraction.
- All statistics are for seed 1 only.

## Values for you to pick

All values are proposals.

| Value | Proposal | Range |
|---|---|---|
| Island cell size | 128 | 96 to 192 |
| Jitter | 0.8 | 0.5 to 1.0 |
| Chance of a cell without an island | 0.1 | 0 to 0.3 |
| Merge chance | 0.3 | 0 to 0.75 |
| Neck exponent `q` | 1 | 1 to 2 |
| Inner cell scale | 0.25 | 0.125 to 0.5 |
| Ripple amplitude | 6 | 2 to 8 |
| Ripple scale | 32 | 16 to 96 |
| Smoothing family | power or exponential | sheet 3 |

The "rocky cubes" selector is the agent's own reading of your words: a box test at the inner cell site with a keep chance.

## State of the four branches

| Branch | State on `main` |
|---|---|
| `claude/bridge-prototype` | You committed it. |
| `claude/islands-ui-mock` | You committed it. |
| `claude/walker-prototype` | Not on `main`. |
| `claude/voronoi-fields` | Not on `main`. |

## Before wave 2

Wave 2 holds the landmass profiles, the asteroid with the lake bowl, and the donut with the biosphere. Its agents copy the field math, so these picks come first:

- the smoothing family;
- the fixes for the smooth max, the profile and the merge;
- the neck exponent `q`;
- the inner cell scale and the meaning of "rocky cubes".

The walker, bridge and editor choices do not block wave 2. They block the Java batches.

### User:

I do not find the walker prototype satisfactory. I was expecting all to random-walk biased upwards in worldspace. Instead the results are too straight and come straight from pillar surface. I was expecting something more like Chorus Plants. I will scrap these from this session instead.

`bridge-prototype` merged in `ab342d47`

* Widening width on rotation is ideal. Right column in `width_railed.png` seems best.
* `heights.png`: Using slabs for island-to-island bridges is ideal. Stairs if slabs otherwise would create a vertical gap. Avoid requiring the player to jump.
* `meet.png` The bridge should not dig into the island. Island blocks win over bridge block placement.


`islands-ui-mock` merged in `c71d2dd6`

* Layout C wins. However, the Island Types are being treated as singletons which isn't right. They are shapes. For example there could be two kinds of asteroids, each with different settings.


Hold wave 2. Looks like there are decisions and problems for me, run me through them please.

```bash
from PIL import Image
def crop(name, w, h, y0, y1, out, maxw=1800):
    im = Image.open('$S/'+name+'.svg.png').convert('RGB')
    s = 3000 / max(w, h)
    ox = (3000 - w * s) / 2; oy = (3000 - h * s) / 2
    c = im.crop((int(ox), int(oy + y0 * s), int(ox + w * s), int(oy + y1 * s)))
    if c.width > maxw:
        c = c.resize((maxw, int(c.height * maxw / c.width)), Image.LANCZOS)
    c.save('$S/'+out); print(out, c.size)
crop('3-smoothing', 1124, 1364, 100, 700, 'fix-wall-normalization.png')
crop('5-two-levels', 1014, 1504, 1200, 1480, 'fix-profile.png')
crop('6-merge', 1240, 2210, 1150, 1410, 'fix-merge-focus-pair.png')
crop('6-merge', 1240, 2210, 1480, 2200, 'fix-merge-gap-and-leak-pairs.png')
"
```

### Claude:

## Your decisions

**Walker trees**
- Walker trees are out of 0.5.0. The plan loses the tree block, the tree feature and the tree chance field of the pillar variation.
- The pillar files stay untouched.
- The branch `claude/walker-prototype` stays for you to delete.

**Voronoi math**

| Point | Decision |
|---|---|
| Smoothing family | The power family only. |
| Wall normalization | A switch of each distance function. |
| Profile | Always `max(0, 1-height)^2`. |
| Radial width | A choice in the data and the editor: the cross section at the origin height, or at the point height. |
| Merge | Drop the shared face only. No threshold fix. |
| "Rocky cubes" | The prototype reading: a box test at the inner cell site, plus a keep chance. |
| Island cell size | An editor setting with the default 256. |
| Other number defaults | You pick them after wave 2. |

**Island model**
- A registry of island shapes holds the density function and the parameter declarations.
- A registry of island types holds a shape, parameter values, the biome list and the structure list. One shape can serve several island types, for example two asteroids.
- A dimension list names island types by id with a weight.
- The editor writes each changed island type as a new entry of the world data pack, with a generated id such as `asteroid_2`.
- A registry `aerolite:island_list` holds the prefab lists.

**Editor**
- Layout C.
- The add row of the type list offers the shipped island types first, then the bare shapes.
- The dimensions have an add row and remove buttons. A dimension keeps its original world generation until the player adds island settings for it.
- A choice parameter is a dropdown, like the pillar shape combo.
- The cloud slider sits in the Overworld header of the panel.
- Each island type entry keeps its own Copy and Paste.
- A fourth group and tab "Bridges" holds the bridge chance, the length limit and the rail switch.

**Bridges**

| Point | Decision |
|---|---|
| Width | The `distance` rule, which widens on rotation. |
| Vertical | Slabs, with stairs where slabs leave a vertical gap. No jump. Slope up to 1.0. |
| Deck meets island | Island blocks win. The deck never digs. |
| End points | The center line. |
| Last step | A ramp of slabs and stairs on top of the island blocks. |
| Merged pair | No bridge. |
| Length limit | A slider in powers of two from 32 to 512, default 128. |
| End stone rail base | End stone bricks. |
| Rail on an island block | An editor switch, default off. |

## Consequences that you did not choose directly

- **Merge gaps stay.** With "drop the shared face only", more than half of the merged pairs keep a gap at fill 0.6. A merged pair gets no bridge, so such a pair has neither a neck nor a bridge.
- **The merge leak does not occur in the game.** Each block reads only the field of its own cell and of the cells merged with it. So no solid appears in a third cell.
- **Cell size 256 against the Overworld height.** The Overworld is 384 blocks high. The cell layers above and below the generating layer lie mostly outside the world. They still shape the cell walls.
- **Structure spacing.** One island cell is 16 chunks wide at size 256. Each cell holds at most one structure unit unless its island type uses inner cells.

## Still open

These points do not block wave 2. They block the Java editor batch:

- the least gap of the two fill bounds;
- the labels "Bottom" and "Top" against "Island Bottom" and "Island Top";
- the weight widget of Layout C, a drag field or a slider;
- the text of the wait screen after Done;
- the clip of the dimension header at 240 units.

## Before wave 2

- `claude/voronoi-fields` is not on `main`. The wave 2 agents read its field math and its GLSL tools.

```bash
git cherry-pick -n main..claude/voronoi-fields
```

- Wave 2 then holds three agents: the landmass profiles, the asteroid with the lake bowl, and the donut with the biosphere.
- Each wave 2 shader uses the power family, the clamped profile and the merge rule "drop the shared face". The wall normalization and the width mode are switches.

```
"""Why "drop the shared face" keeps a gap: which other face stops each island before the shared wall."""
import sys

import numpy as np

sys.path.insert(0, sys.argv[1])
import fields as F  # noqa: E402

world = F.World(F.SEED)
fill = F.FILL


def report(a_idx, b_idx):
    a, b = world.cell(a_idx), world.cell(b_idx)
    print(f"pair {a.idx} {b.idx}")
    for own, other in ((a, b), (b, a)):
        face = [f for f in own.faces if f["nbr"] == other.idx][0]
        V = face["verts"]
        area = 0.0
        for i in range(1, len(V) - 1):
            area += np.linalg.norm(np.cross(V[i] - V[0], V[i + 1] - V[0])) / 2
        centroid = V.mean(0)
        keep = np.array([nb != other.idx for nb in own.nbrs])
        nbrs = [nb for nb in own.nbrs if nb != other.idx]
        # points on the shared face: lowest max over the other faces
        W = []
        k = 40
        for i in range(1, len(V) - 1):
            for p in range(k + 1):
                for q in range(k + 1 - p):
                    W.append((k - p - q) / k * V[0] + p / k * V[i] + q / k * V[i + 1])
        W = np.array(W)
        t, D = F.t_values(own, own.site, W, keep)
        g = t.max(-1)
        best = int(g.argmin())
        blocker = nbrs[int(t[best].argmax())]
        d_shared = face["c"] - face["n"] @ own.site
        print(f"  cell {own.idx}: shared face area {area:7.0f}, site to shared wall {d_shared:5.1f} blocks, "
              f"faces {len(own.faces)}")
        print(f"    lowest field value on the shared face without that face: {g.min():.3f} "
              f"(solid needs < {fill}), limited by the face toward {blocker}")
        print(f"    share of the shared face that is solid: {(g < fill).mean() * 100:.1f}%")
        # walk from the site to the best point and name the face that stops the island
        u = np.linspace(0, 1, 2001)[:, None]
        P = own.site + u * (W[best] - own.site)
        tt, _ = F.t_values(own, own.site, P, keep)
        gg = tt.max(-1)
        stop = np.nonzero(gg >= fill)[0]
        if len(stop):
            s = stop[0]
            print(f"    on the way from the site to that point, the island ends at {u[s, 0] * 100:.0f}% of the way, "
                  f"at the scaled face toward {nbrs[int(tt[s].argmax())]}")


report((0, 0, 0), (1, 0, 0))
report((-4, 0, -4), (-3, 0, -4))
```

### Claude:

Row 4 column 2 has no gap. That pair joins. The gap is in row 5 column 2 of the same sheet, the pair of the cells (-4, 0, -4) and (-3, 0, -4).

## Why a gap can stay

- The island of a cell is the cell shrunk to the fill fraction around its site. At fill 0.6, each wall of the island sits at 60% of the distance from the site to the cell wall.
- "Drop the shared face" removes only the island wall toward the merged neighbor. Every other island wall stays at 60%.
- So the island grows toward the neighbor only until it meets one of its other walls.
- A point on the shared face is solid only if it lies inside the 60% position of every other face of the cell.
- A shared face that is small, or that lies close to other faces, is completely outside that zone. Then no island reaches it.

## The two pairs, measured

I ran the cell code of the prototype on both pairs. The field value on the shared face must be below 0.6 for a solid block.

| Pair | Lowest field value on the shared face | Share of the shared face that is solid |
|---|---|---|
| Row 4, cell (0, 0, 0) | 0.256 | 14.1% |
| Row 4, cell (1, 0, 0) | 0.502 | 7.4% |
| Row 5, cell (-4, 0, -4) | 0.629 | 0% |
| Row 5, cell (-3, 0, -4) | 0.644 | 0% |

- In row 5, both islands stop at their wall toward the cell (-3, -1, -4). That cell is in the layer below the generating layer.
- The left island ends at 96% of the way from its site to the shared face. The right island ends at 93%.
- So this pair joins at a fill above 0.644. It keeps a gap at 0.6.
- The panel is a horizontal slice at Y 76. The wall that stops the islands belongs to a cell below, so the slice does not show it as a line between the islands.

## The gap depends on the fill fraction

I ran the pair count of the prototype at five fill fractions, on seed 1 with 217 merged pairs.

| Fill fraction | Merged pairs that keep a gap |
|---|---|
| 0.3 | 198 (91%) |
| 0.45 | 171 (79%) |
| 0.6 | 120 (55%) |
| 0.75 | 69 (32%) |
| 0.9 | 31 (14%) |

- The count tests sample points on the shared face. A very thin contact between the sample points can be missed.
- The fill fraction of each island is random between the two bounds, 0.3 and 0.9 by default. So most merged pairs with a low fill stay apart.
- A merged pair gets no bridge by your decision. So a merged pair with a gap has no neck and no bridge.

```
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Exports the cell pairs of sheet 6 for the 3D view of the merge in `gap3d/index.html`.

The script writes `gap3d/data.js`. Each pair holds the two real 3D cells: site, face planes and face vertices.

    python3 prototypes/voronoi/gap3d.py
    python3 prototypes/voronoi/gap3d.py --seed 7

Written by Claude Code; conversation transcript:
llm/transcripts/transcript_2026-11-06_06-10_db4fad62-80be-4308-94eb-dd6b2e208826.md
"""
import argparse
import json
import pathlib

import fields as F

HERE = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parent
OUT = HERE / "gap3d" / "data.js"

# The pairs of sheet 6 never show more than this many blocked pairs in the list.
EXTRA_GAP_PAIRS = 4


def cell_record(cell):
    return {
        "idx": list(cell.idx),
        "site": [round(float(v), 4) for v in cell.site],
        "faces": [
            {
                "n": [round(float(v), 6) for v in f["n"]],
                "c": round(float(f["c"]), 4),
                "nbr": list(f["nbr"]),
                "verts": [[round(float(v), 4) for v in p] for p in f["verts"]],
            }
            for f in cell.faces
        ],
    }


def pair_record(name, a, b):
    return {"name": name, "a": cell_record(a), "b": cell_record(b)}


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=F.SEED)
    args = parser.parse_args()

    world = F.World(args.seed)
    focus = world.focus()
    stats = F.merge_stats(world)
    blocked = [r for r in stats if not r[3]]
    leaks = [r for r in stats if r[2] is not None]

    pairs = []
    a, b = F.pair_cells(world, focus)[:2]
    pairs.append(pair_record("Row 4: the focus pair", a, b))
    if blocked:
        pairs.append(pair_record("Row 5: a pair with a gap at fill 0.6", blocked[0][0], blocked[0][1]))
    if leaks:
        pairs.append(pair_record("Row 6: the pair that leaks on the sheet", leaks[0][0], leaks[0][1]))
    for r in blocked[1:1 + EXTRA_GAP_PAIRS]:
        pairs.append(pair_record(f"Gap pair {r[0].idx} {r[1].idx}", r[0], r[1]))

    data = {"seed": args.seed, "cell": F.CELL, "jitter": F.JITTER, "fill": F.FILL, "pairs": pairs}
    OUT.parent.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    OUT.write_text("// Written by gap3d.py. Do not edit.\nvar GAP3D = " + json.dumps(data, separators=(",", ":")) + ";\n")
    print(f"{OUT.relative_to(HERE)}: {len(pairs)} pairs, seed {args.seed}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

</details>

```
// 3D view of the neighbor merge rule "drop the shared face". The page reads GAP3D from data.js.
// Written by Claude Code; conversation transcript:
// llm/transcripts/transcript_2026-11-06_06-10_db4fad62-80be-4308-94eb-dd6b2e208826.md
(function () {
  'use strict';

  var EPS = 1e-6;
  var FAR = 4000;

  // ---- vectors ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  function dot(a, b) { return a[0] * b[0] + a[1] * b[1] + a[2] * b[2]; }
  function add(a, b) { return [a[0] + b[0], a[1] + b[1], a[2] + b[2]]; }
  function sub(a, b) { return [a[0] - b[0], a[1] - b[1], a[2] - b[2]]; }
  function mul(a, s) { return [a[0] * s, a[1] * s, a[2] * s]; }
  function cross(a, b) { return [a[1] * b[2] - a[2] * b[1], a[2] * b[0] - a[0] * b[2], a[0] * b[1] - a[1] * b[0]]; }
  function unit(a) { var l = Math.sqrt(dot(a, a)); return mul(a, 1 / l); }
  function same(a, b) { return a[0] === b[0] && a[1] === b[1] && a[2] === b[2]; }

  // ---- convex geometry ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  // A plane is {n, c, tag}. A point x is inside when n . x <= c.

  /** Keeps the part of a convex polygon inside one plane. */
  function clipPolygon(poly, plane) {
    var out = [];
    for (var i = 0; i < poly.length; i++) {
      var p = poly[i], q = poly[(i + 1) % poly.length];
      var dp = dot(plane.n, p) - plane.c, dq = dot(plane.n, q) - plane.c;
      if (dp <= EPS) out.push(p);
      if ((dp < -EPS && dq > EPS) || (dp > EPS && dq < -EPS)) out.push(add(p, mul(sub(q, p), dp / (dp - dq))));
    }
    return out;
  }

  function clipAll(poly, planes, skip) {
    for (var j = 0; j < planes.length && poly.length >= 3; j++) {
      if (j !== skip) poly = clipPolygon(poly, planes[j]);
    }
    return poly;
  }

  function area(poly) {
    var s = [0, 0, 0];
    for (var i = 1; i + 1 < poly.length; i++) s = add(s, cross(sub(poly[i], poly[0]), sub(poly[i + 1], poly[0])));
    return Math.sqrt(dot(s, s)) / 2;
  }

  /** The faces of the convex solid inside every plane. Each face keeps the tag of its plane. */
  function polytope(planes) {
    var faces = [];
    for (var i = 0; i < planes.length; i++) {
      var n = planes[i].n;
      var u = unit(cross(n, Math.abs(n[1]) < 0.9 ? [0, 1, 0] : [1, 0, 0]));
      var v = cross(n, u);
      var o = mul(n, planes[i].c);
      var quad = [
        add(o, add(mul(u, -FAR), mul(v, -FAR))), add(o, add(mul(u, FAR), mul(v, -FAR))),
        add(o, add(mul(u, FAR), mul(v, FAR))), add(o, add(mul(u, -FAR), mul(v, FAR)))
      ];
      var poly = clipAll(quad, planes, i);
      if (poly.length >= 3 && area(poly) > 1e-3) faces.push({ verts: poly, tag: planes[i].tag });
    }
    return faces;
  }

  // ---- the merge rule -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  function cellPlanes(cell) {
    return cell.faces.map(function (f) { return { n: f.n, c: f.c, tag: null }; });
  }

  /** The island walls of one cell at a fill fraction. Each wall sits at that fraction of the way to its cell wall. */
  function islandPlanes(cell, other, fill, drop) {
    var planes = [];
    cell.faces.forEach(function (f) {
      if (drop && same(f.nbr, other.idx)) return;
      var base = dot(f.n, cell.site);
      planes.push({ n: f.n, c: base + fill * (f.c - base), tag: f.nbr });
    });
    return planes;
  }

  function sharedFace(cell, other) {
    for (var i = 0; i < cell.faces.length; i++) if (same(cell.faces[i].nbr, other.idx)) return cell.faces[i];
    return null;
  }

  /** The lowest field value of a cell on the shared face, without the shared face, and the face that sets it. */
  function lowestOnShared(cell, other) {
    var face = sharedFace(cell, other), V = face.verts, k = 36, best = { g: Infinity, nbr: null, p: null };
    var terms = cell.faces.filter(function (f) { return !same(f.nbr, other.idx); }).map(function (f) {
      return { n: f.n, d: f.c - dot(f.n, cell.site), nbr: f.nbr };
    })
… [9850 more characters omitted]
```

```
      wall toward the merged neighbor. So the island grows toward the neighbor until it meets one of its other walls.

    </div>

      <span class="key"><span class="sw" style="background:#ffe14a"></span>Solid part of the shared face</span>
      <span class="key"><span class="sw" style="background:#e0661f"></span>Limiting wall: on the island, and its face on the cell</span>
      <span class="key"><span class="sw" style="background:#202020"></span>Cell walls and sites</span>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div id="view"></div>
</div>
<script src="../../shapes/vendor/three.min.js"></script>
```

```
## 3D view of the merge

`gap3d/index.html` shows the merge rule "drop the shared face" on real 3D cells.

- Open the page from the file system in a browser, for example `open prototypes/voronoi/gap3d/index.html` on macOS.
- The page needs no build step and no network. It loads three.js from `../shapes/vendor/`.
- `gap3d.py` writes `gap3d/data.js`. The data holds the three pairs of rows 4 to 6 of sheet 6 and four more pairs with a gap.
- `gap3d/gap3d.js` builds each island as a convex solid from the cell planes.
- An island is its cell, scaled to the fill fraction around its site.
- The merge rule removes only the island wall toward the merged neighbor.
- So the island grows toward the neighbor until it meets one of its other walls.
- The field of a cell holds only inside the two merged cells. So the page shows no solid in a third cell.
- A drag turns the view. The scroll wheel zooms. A right drag pans.
- The URL takes `?pair=1&fill=0.6`.

| Control | Effect |
|---|---|
| Pair | The pair of cells. |
| Fill | The fill fraction, 0.30 to 0.99. |
| Drop the shared face | Off shows the gap case. Both islands keep every wall. |
| Cell walls | The edges of both cells. |
| Mark the limiting wall | Orange marks the island wall that sets the lowest field value on the shared face, and its face on the cell. |
| Island opacity | The opacity of both islands. |

| Color | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Green | The island of the first cell. |
| Tan | The island of the second cell. |
| Blue | The shared face. |
| Yellow | The part of the shared face that is solid. No yellow means a gap. |
| Orange | The limiting wall. |

- The line under the controls gives the lowest field value of each cell on the shared face. An island reaches the shared face at a fill above that value.
- The solid share on the page is an exact area. The statistics of sheet 6 use sample points.
- The page does not show a solid outside both cells. Row 6 of sheet 6 shows that case.
```

The 3D view is live in the Browser pane. It opens on the row 5 pair at fill 0.60.

## What the page shows

- Green and tan are the two islands. Each island is its real 3D cell, scaled to the fill fraction around its site.
- Blue is the shared face of the two cells.
- Yellow is the part of the shared face that is solid. No yellow means a gap.
- Orange is the island wall that stops the island before the shared face. The same orange marks the cell face that this wall belongs to.
- The black lines are the edges of both cells.

A drag turns the view. The scroll wheel zooms. A right drag pans.

## What to try

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| Keep the pair "Row 5" at fill 0.60 | The blue face lies in the open space between the islands. No island reaches it. |
| Move Fill up slowly | The green island reaches the blue face above 0.627. The tan island reaches it above 0.645. |
| Set Fill to 0.75 | Both islands pass through the shared face. The solid share of the face is 14.1% and 11.7%. |
| Turn "Drop the shared face" off | Both islands keep every wall. This is the gap case of column 1. |
| Select the pair "Row 4" | The islands join at fill 0.60. This is the panel that you looked at. |

## Why the row 5 pair keeps a gap

- The orange wall is the top wall of each island. It belongs to the face toward the cell (-3, 1, -4) in the layer above.
- The shared face of this pair sits high in both cells, above that top wall at fill 0.60.
- The merge rule removes only the island wall toward the neighbor. The top wall stays, so each island ends below the shared face.

## Checks and limits

- The page has no console error.
- I compared its numbers with the Python code of the prototype. The lowest field values agree to 0.002.
- The solid share on the page is an exact area. The sheet statistics use sample points, so the row 4 share differs: 23.6% on the page against 14.1% from the samples.
- The page shows no solid outside the two merged cells. Row 6 of the sheet shows that case.
- The data also holds four more pairs with a gap.

## Files

The commit `8a1670c` is on the branch `claude/merge-gap-3d`. I removed the worktree.


## Decisions of this round

| Point | Decision |
|---|---|
| Merge rule | "Drop the shared face only" stays. |
| Merged pair with a gap | It rolls the bridge chance of the dimension, like any neighbor pair. |
| Gap test | The deck line test. The bridge step follows the center line between the two cell centers. Air between the two islands on that line means a gap. |
| Wave 2 form | A local viewer page comes first. The three shape batches start after it. |
| Radial width mode | A field of the function. A shape binds it to a choice parameter when it wants a dropdown. |

## Wave 2, new sequence

| Step | Batch | Folder |
|---|---|---|
| 2a | Local WebGL viewer: it runs any Shadertoy port from the file system, with an orbit camera and one control per switch. | `prototypes/tools/` |
| 2b | Landmass profiles: Flat, Hills, Mountain, Mesa steps. | `prototypes/landmass/` |
| 2b | Asteroid and lake bowl. | `prototypes/asteroid/`, `prototypes/lake-bowl/` |
| 2b | Donut and biosphere. | `prototypes/donut/`, `prototypes/biosphere/` |

## Decisions that come next

**After wave 2, from the viewer and the renders**
- The number defaults: jitter, the chance of a cell without an island, the merge chance, the inner cell scale, the ripple amplitude and the ripple scale.

**Before the Java batches start**
- Structures: where the needed radius of a structure set lives, the island type entry or a data map.

- Biomes: the names of the climate groups, the scale of the region noise, and the biome list of each shipped island type.
- Bridges: the greatest length of the ramp on the island, and the result when the ramp does not fit.
- Placement: the island height band of the Nether and of the End.
- Editor: the five small points from the mock.

### Claude:

The shader viewer is complete and checked. The three shape batches now run on top of it, and no shape result exists yet.

## Viewer: `claude/shader-viewer`

- `python3 prototypes/tools/glsl.py check prototypes/voronoi_fields.frag` passes all six checks.
- I opened the page in the Browser pane on the real GPU. It renders the islands at 78 frames per second at render scale 50%, with no console error.
- A drag and the scroll wheel changed the camera values in the URL. I have no picture after the drag, because the pane was hidden at that time.
- The agent tested the page from a `file://` URL in a headless Chromium. I did not repeat that test.
- The commit holds only its own paths, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree, stopped the server and deleted the launch file. I also closed eight dead tabs that earlier agents left in the pane.

```bash
git cherry-pick -n main..claude/shader-viewer
```

After the cherry-pick, the page opens from the repository root:

```bash
open -a "Google Chrome" "file://$PWD/prototypes/tools/viewer/index.html?port=voronoi_fields"
```

## What the viewer does

| Item | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Port | It loads a port by name, from a file picker, or from a dropped file. |
| Camera | A drag turns the view through `iMouse`. The scroll wheel zooms with no new compile. |
| Controls | `voronoi_fields` shows 26 controls: 7 dropdowns and 19 sliders. |
| Values | A box lists the changed `#define` lines, so you can copy your values. |
| State | The URL holds the port, the camera and the changed values. |

`voronoi_fields` now follows your decisions: `MERGE 1` drops the shared face only, `MERGE 2` keeps the threshold variant for comparison, and `CELL` defaults to 256.

## Open problem in `voronoi_fields`

- Thin slivers of solid show near merged faces with `MERGE 1` and `MERGE 2`.
- A tighter march shows more of them, so they are real solid, not march noise.
- The agent did not find the cause. It is the merge rule itself, or the 56-plane shortcut of the shader.
- I did not examine it further.

## Shape batches, running now

| Branch | Shapes |
|---|---|
| `claude/landmass-shapes` | Flat, Hills, Mountain, Mesa steps |
| `claude/asteroid-lake-shapes` | Asteroid, Lake bowl |
| `claude/donut-biosphere-shapes` | Donut, Biosphere |

- Each branch starts from `claude/shader-viewer`, because the viewer is not on `main`.
- Each shape is one `.frag` file with its port, its viewer wrapper, a README with the density formula, and rendered images.
- Each shape declares its named parameters as viewer controls. Each open point is a dropdown, and none is marked as preferred.

I read the file names from the Wikimedia category pages. I did not view the images.

- [Chocolate Hills, Bohol](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Chocolate_Hills_overview.JPG): rounded hills, for the Hills shape.
- [Grand Staircase, Utah](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Escalante,_Grand_Staircase,_Utah.jpg): stepped cliffs, for the Mesa steps shape.
- [Atafu, Tokelau](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Atafu.jpg): atoll ring, for the Donut shape.
- A biosphere has no Earth landform.

### Claude:

The landmass shapes are complete and checked. The other two shape batches still run.

## Landmass: `claude/landmass-shapes`

- `python3 prototypes/tools/glsl.py check prototypes/landmass_*.frag` passes all 24 checks.
- I looked at four of the 28 renders: the Mountain and the Flat from above, the Mesa steps from below, and the Hills with inner cells. Each one shows its shape with a clear top and underside.
- The commit holds only its own paths, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree. The branch stays.
- I did not open the four ports in the viewer on the real GPU. The agent opened them in a headless Chromium and saw every control. No one tested a drag or a slider there.

You merged the viewer as `c4b0d69`, and the branch `claude/shader-viewer` is gone. The landmass branch holds one commit on top of the old viewer commit. So the cherry-pick takes that one commit:

```bash
git cherry-pick -n claude/landmass-shapes
```

After the cherry-pick, each shape opens in the viewer:

```bash
open -a "Google Chrome" "file://$PWD/prototypes/tools/viewer/index.html?port=landmass_mountain"
```

The other ports are `landmass_flat`, `landmass_hills` and `landmass_mesa`.

## The four shapes

All shapes use the radial distance and the vertical distance, both divided by the fill fraction of the island.

| Shape | Top | Underside | Parameters |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flat | A level plateau with a steep edge and light roughness. | Beard or mirror, by dropdown. | Height, Steepness, Roughness, Depth, Beard roughness |
| Hills | A dome with rolling noise. | Beard or lens, by dropdown. | Height, Relief, Hill size, Depth, Beard roughness |
| Mountain | Your formula with the clamp, plus ridge noise that fades at the peak. | The same shape upside down, with its own depth. | Height, Depth, Jaggedness, Jag size |
| Mesa steps | Terraces from a sum of clamps, with a wobble on the edges. | Beard or stepped, by dropdown. | Height, Terraces, Cliff, Wobble, Depth, Beard roughness |

At Height 1, Depth 1, Jaggedness 0 and fill 1, the Mountain is your formula exactly.

## Your choices

**All shapes**
- One fill fraction per merged cluster, or one per cell. With one per cell, two merged islands meet with a step.
- The height in filled cell heights, or in blocks.
- The fill fraction scales the height and the radius, or the radius only.
- Inner cells replace the shape (`INNER 1`), or clip it (`INNER 2`).

**Per shape**
- Flat: beard or mirror, a sharp or a rounded plateau edge, roughness on the cliffs or not.
- Hills: beard or lens, plain noise or separate knolls.
- Mountain: a separate depth or the depth equal to the height, and the width mode. The width mode changes this shape the most.
- Mesa steps: beard or stepped, equal or seeded terrace heights, one wobble for all steps or one per step.

Every number is a proposal.

## Problems that the agent found

- **The Docker renderer has a loop limit.** The first renders showed shards and speckles where a pixel ran more than 65535 loop steps. The agent cut the loop steps, and the renders are clean now. The link to the loop limit of the Mesa renderer is the agent's inference.
- **A browser on a GPU has no such limit.** So a port can work in the viewer and still break in `glsl.py render`.
- **This can explain the slivers of `voronoi_fields`.** No one tested that link.
- **Cracks of sky along cell walls.** The step cap of `voronoi_fields` caused them. The landmass shaders use a new cap at the cell exit along the ray, and the cracks are gone. `voronoi_fields` still has the old cap.
- **The four files share most of their code.** A change to the shared Voronoi code must go into all four files.

## Limits

- The noise is a stand-in for the vanilla noise.
- The density function trees exist only as formulas in the README. No JSON and no Java exists.
- The Hills render with inner cells shows speckles on the far islands. I did not examine them.

### Claude:

The donut and biosphere shapes are complete and checked. The asteroid and lake bowl batch still runs.

## Donut and biosphere: `claude/donut-biosphere-shapes`

- `python3 prototypes/tools/glsl.py check` passes all 12 checks for both files.
- `python3 prototypes/biosphere/shell_check.py` reports 0 leaked blocks through the one-block glass band.
- I looked at three of the 32 renders: the donut default, the biosphere default and the hanging garden. Each one shows its shape.
- The commit holds only its own paths, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree. The branch stays.
- I did not open the two ports in the viewer on the real GPU. The agent opened them in a headless Chromium and saw every control.

The branch holds one commit on top of the old viewer commit:

```bash
git cherry-pick -n claude/donut-biosphere-shapes
```

The ports are `donut` and `biosphere`:

```bash
open -a "Google Chrome" "file://$PWD/prototypes/tools/viewer/index.html?port=biosphere"
```

## The two shapes

| Shape | Form | Parameters |
|---|---|---|
| Donut | A ring around a seeded axis per cell. The hole fraction is drawn per cell. The profile is a round tube or a landmass with a flat top and a beard. | Hole min, Hole max, Max tilt, Profile, Top height, Beard depth, Roughness |
| Biosphere | A dome field with a one-block glass shell and terrain inside. The dome is a sphere, one of five Platonic solids, the Voronoi cell or a horizontal donut. | Dome, Terrain, Ground height, Roughness, Hole min, Hole max |

- The glass color follows the CLAUDE.md weights: clear 32, each stained glass 1, and tinted glass for a cave biome.
- Hanging terrain mirrors the ground terrain onto the dome ceiling.
- Each README gives three example island types as viewer URLs.

## What I saw in the renders

- **Donut default:** Max tilt defaults to 90 degrees, so many rings stand on edge. In the normalized unit, a ring follows its cell and looks like a polygon.
- **Biosphere default:** the Platonic solids are small, and the Voronoi cell dome is much larger than the other domes. The size rule fits each dome inside the largest sphere of its cell.

## Your choices

**Donut**
- The unit: a polygon ring that follows the cell, or a round ring.
- The profile, the width mode and the range of the tilt.
- The inner cell mode and its scale. The agent proposes 0.06.
- Merge on donuts. It gives flat cuts at the shared faces.

**Biosphere**
- The weights of the dome draw.
- The pose of the Platonic solids: one face down with a seeded yaw, or a full rotation.
- The dome size rule.
- An exact one-block shell for the Voronoi cell dome.
- A separate ceiling height for hanging terrain.

## Additions that both shapes need from the Voronoi source

These are new requirements for the Java batches:

- a seeded frame per cell, for the ring axis and the pose of a solid;
- the smallest wall distance from the origin of a cell;
- more seeded random values per cell;
- a spline for one over the tube radius, because no density function divides by another.

## Problems and limits

- **Smoothing with merge on the donut.** Two merged rings meet the shared face at different heights. A 2000-step march shows the same gap, so it is geometry.
- **The same march problem as the landmass batch.** The agent found the crack and the specks from the old step cap and fixed them the same way, by its own work. The two fixes are separate copies.
- **Not checked:** no script tests the rules that keep a shape inside its cell. They hold by the math only. The glass check did not sample the Voronoi cell dome.
- Neither shader has the wall normalization.

## State

| Branch | State |
|---|---|
| `claude/landmass-shapes` | You committed it as `0dd412a`. |
| `claude/donut-biosphere-shapes` | Checked. Not on `main`. |
| `claude/asteroid-lake-shapes` | Its agent still runs. |

## Asteroid and lake bowl: `claude/asteroid-lake-shapes`

- `python3 prototypes/tools/glsl.py check` passes all 12 checks for both files.
- I looked at three of the 30 Docker renders: the lake bowl at tilt 15, the coconut bowl and the cobble asteroid. Each one shows its shape, and the water lies inside the bowls.
- The commit holds only its own paths, and it has no forbidden word.
- I removed the worktree. The branch stays.

```bash
git cherry-pick -n claude/asteroid-lake-shapes
```

## The lake bowl defect

| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| Docker renderer | Good pictures. All 18 images come from it. |
| Headless Chromium with software WebGL (the agent) | The port compiles and shows 33 controls. Then the WebGL context is lost on the first frame. |
| Headless Chrome on this Mac, default backend (me) | The canvas shows the lost-context icon. |
| Headless Chrome on this Mac, Metal backend (me) | One run lost the GPU process. One run stayed at "Compiling..." after 25 seconds. |
| Browser pane of the app (me) | The page reports "Compiled" and no lost context. The pane was hidden, so it drew no frame, and I have no picture. |

- The asteroid port renders in the same headless Chrome, at 11 frames per second.
- So the lake bowl shader is too heavy for the browser as it is. It has 700 lines and several nested loops.
- No one has seen a lake bowl frame in a browser.

## The two shapes

| Shape | Form | Parameters |
|---|---|---|
| Asteroid | The cell distance with power smoothing and noise. With inner cells, each solid inner cell is a blob, and the blobs join by a hard or a smooth union. | Roundness, Lumpiness, Lump size, Inner cell size, Inner cell fill, Join width, Combine |
| Lake bowl | An outer body, a cavity, a tilted cut plane and a water body below a level per island. The body is the Voronoi cell, a Platonic solid or a half blob of inner cells. | Shape, Solid, Tilt, Rim height, Shell thickness, Roundness, Cavity, Water margin, Water level rule |

## Your choices

**Asteroid**
- Inner cells on or off, their size, and a hard or a smooth join.
- The combine mode: inner cells alone, union with the rock, intersection, or rock minus crater cells.
- Merge for asteroids. A merged asteroid ends in a flat cut against a smaller neighbor.

**Lake bowl**
- Which bowl shapes ship, and their weights.
- The cavity: the scaled shape, or a ball. The ball gives small round holes.
- The water level rule. The first rule keeps the water below the opening. It does not prove a wall of one block next to the lowest rim point. The second rule proves that wall, and it leaves the half blobs with no water in the shader.
- Who computes the water level: an exact clip of the cavity in Java, or the bound of the shader. The bound puts the water about 11 blocks lower than necessary at tilt 30.

## New requirements for the Java batches

The donut, the biosphere and the lake bowl need more from the Voronoi source:

- a seeded frame per cell, for a ring axis, a solid pose and a tilted cut plane;
- the smallest wall distance from the origin of a cell;
- more seeded random values per cell;
- a spline for a division, because no density function divides by another;
- the water level per island.

```
## 1. State

## 2. How the owner works

- A plan request means questions. Use AskUserQuestion until the owner has chosen every detail. Present no option as recommended.

- Each open rule of a prototype becomes a labeled variant or a dropdown. The owner picks.
```

## Lake bowl fix: `claude/lake-bowl-fix`

- One Opus agent works on top of `claude/asteroid-lake-shapes`.
- Its task: find the real cause of the lost WebGL context, then make the port render in Chrome on this Mac at an interactive frame time.
- It keeps the shape math, the water rule and the documented controls.
- It also writes `prototypes/tools/viewer_shot.py`. That tool opens a viewer URL in Chrome, waits in real time, and saves a screenshot with the frame time.
- It measures every other port with that tool and reports the numbers. It does not change the other ports.

## What I tested before the fix

- In a headless Chrome on this Mac, `voronoi_fields`, `asteroid`, `biosphere` and `landmass_flat` render.
- `lake_bowl` lost the GPU process in three of five runs.
- Two of my test modes were not valid, and I discarded their results. The agent has those lessons.

## Handoff document

- The draft is `llm/handoffs/0.5.0-islands.md` on the branch `claude/islands-handoff`. It is not committed yet.
- It holds the state of each branch, your decisions by area, the open decisions, and the notes for the Java implementation.
- The Java notes name the reference implementations in the prototypes, the Aerolite classes to follow, and the vanilla facts with their classes.
- It names the prior art that Aerolite must not copy or port.
- One section waits for the result of the fix.

## What happens next

- The agent reports. Then I check its branch, its screenshots and its frame times.
- I write the result into the handoff, commit the handoff, remove both worktrees, and give you the cherry-pick commands.

```bash
### Cause

The batch found four causes, in this sequence:

1. The Metal backend rejected the shader: "Fragment function exceeds available stack space". The shader held three local arrays of 56 faces and passed two of them by value. Metal inlines each call, and the shader called its heavy functions from many sites.
2. Without the arrays, the Metal pipeline took about 15 seconds to build at the first draw. The first frame then failed with "Metal command buffer execution failed".
3. In this backend, `pow`, a division and `log2` are slow, and `exp2` is fast. A loop over the 5x5x5 block with a test costs more than a loop over a table of the 56 offsets.
4. The longest rays lose the context, whatever the frame time. A 550 ms frame ran. A ray of more than about 300 to 400 steps did not. The mechanism is unknown.

### Changes

- No face arrays. A function computes face `i` from its index.
- Each heavy function has one call site. Lo
```

### Claude:

The fix batch is done and checked, and the handoff is committed. Nothing is on `main`.

## Lake bowl fix: `claude/lake-bowl-fix`

- The port `lake_bowl` now renders in headless Chrome on this Mac, on ANGLE with Metal.
- I ran the default view and the half blob view myself. Each drew 65 frames at about 200 ms per frame with no lost context.
- The GLSL check passes. The commit touches only the lake bowl files and one new tool.
- The shape math, the water rule and the controls stay. The march now takes a least step of a quarter pixel, so far silhouettes differ slightly from the old images.
- The cause was the Metal backend, in four parts:
  1. Three local arrays of 56 faces exceeded the stack of the fragment function.
  2. Without the arrays, the pipeline took about 15 seconds to build, and the first frame failed.
  3. `pow` and divisions are slow in this backend.
  4. Rays above about 300 to 400 steps lose the context, whatever the frame time. The mechanism is unknown.
- The new tool `prototypes/tools/viewer_shot.py` runs a viewer URL in headless Chrome with a real wait. It saves a screenshot and prints the frame time and the lost context flag.

## What still fails

- The asteroid examples "cobble" and "cratered" of its README lose the WebGL context in Chrome. The default asteroid runs.
- The lake bowl with `ORIGIN=1` loses the context after 4 frames. I saw the same in my run.
- The viewer shows "Compiled." before Metal builds the pipeline, so the status comes too early for a heavy port.
- No port ran in a visible Chrome window, in Safari or in Firefox. Each number comes from headless Chrome on this Mac.

## Handoff: `claude/islands-handoff`

The document is `llm/handoffs/0.5.0-islands.md`. It holds:

- The state of each commit and branch.
- Your decisions by area, and the open decisions that you have not picked.
- The notes for the Java implementation: the reference implementations in the prototypes, the Aerolite classes to follow, the vanilla facts, the risks and the tests.
- The Java batch outline, the prior art that Aerolite must not copy, the result of the fix, and the tool commands.

No Java batch started. The handoff states that each wave needs your explicit approval.

```bash

```

The handoff document:

```bash

```
